# Identity inventory

Snapshot taken before independence edits on 2026-11-19. Every matching tracked text line is recorded below. Searches cover case-insensitive `sub2api`, `Sub2API`, `s2a`, `.s2plugin` and `Wei-Shaw`. `.git`, dependencies, LICENSE, NOTICE and legal directories are excluded. Line references describe this snapshot and will move as files change.

## Classification

- **a** covers current display and build identity.
- **b** covers persisted, configuration and protocol identity requiring compatibility.
- **c** covers legal/provenance material and incidental matches inside opaque asset/checksum bytes.

## Versioned migration policy

The independence transition is identity migration v1. New operational names use `omnihive` and `OMNIHIVE_*`. Readers accept documented legacy names and emit deprecation diagnostics. Explicit new configuration wins, including explicitly empty environment values. Existing database names, external volume names, cryptographic salts, queued work and distributed coordination keys require a deployment-wide migration after old instances drain. The target exit window is two stable releases or about 90 days after the independent stable release, with the actual removal gated by the deployed-instance inventory.

## Plugin and protocol migration design

Plugin packages retain their v1 bytes and signature verification. The separate v2 importer must verify the original manifest and archive first, enforce publisher/capability checks, map the historical host version range to a tested OmniHive range, generate `.ohplugin` with a v2 manifest, and obtain a new signature from an authorized publisher. Record original/new digests and signer IDs. The SDK moves to `omnihive.plugin.v2` with a new handshake and regenerated clients. The iframe bridge uses a versioned readiness event. New trust roots need signed trust metadata from the existing trusted release chain or explicit operator verification of publisher and pinned digest. Packages cannot supply their own trusted root. Publish a compatibility report and rollback mapping before enabling v2.

Cryptographic salts, fingerprint namespaces, TOTP secrets, identity tickets, Redis locks/queues, prompt-audit payloads and SQL settings retain their established values until a dedicated dual-read and coordinated-drain migration is tested. Public protocol aliases require old endpoint/header support during the same window. The frontend owner handles auth refresh locks, storage keys, export formats and plugin administration fields.

| `frontend/src/stores/auth.ts:19`, `frontend/src/api/auth.ts:72`, `frontend/src/api/client.ts:78`, `frontend/src/api/tokenRefresh.ts:9`, `frontend/src/api/tokenRefresh.ts:28` | b | Migrate access and refresh token storage, expiry, user metadata, pending identity sessions and refresh lock as one versioned contract. Keep mixed-version tabs coordinated until they drain. |
| `backend/internal/handler/auth_oidc_oauth.go:37` and the provider-specific OAuth handlers | b | Preserve active OAuth callback cookies and secrets across upgrades. Age out old cookies before renaming any key. |
| `backend/internal/handler/admin/account_data.go:23`, `frontend/src/views/admin/AccountsView.vue` | b | Coordinate the accepted export discriminator, filenames, and versioned backups with the console importer. |
| `backend/internal/server/middleware/admin_auth.go:39`, `backend/internal/handler/admin/ops_ws_handler.go:53` | b | Negotiate the new and legacy WebSocket subprotocols before changing the console client. Continue stripping short-lived ticket values from handshake responses. |
